# Interoperability report

Generated by `go run ./interop` on 2026-11-14 14:22 UTC. Do not edit by hand: rerun the command.

Every JPEG and PNG fixture in `testdata/` is validated by each tool in two trust modes, together with each unsigned fixture signed by C2paSeal in five ways:

- `c2paseal/…`: plain; `c2paseal+tsa/…`: with an RFC 3161 time-stamp from a local test TSA;
- `c2paseal+parent/…`: the `c2paseal/…` file signed again, so it carries two manifests and records the first as its parent ingredient;
- `c2paseal+sidecar/…`: the manifest store in a `.c2pa` sidecar next to the file;
- `c2paseal+remote/…`: the store only at the URL in the file's XMP.

Remote stores (these and the c2patool fixtures in `testdata/remote/`) are served by the harness on `http://127.0.0.1:8765/` while it runs. The trust modes:

- **default**: the tool's own default trust (C2paSeal and c2patool: no anchors; c2pa-mcp: its embedded C2PA trust list; Credentio: no anchors, and it then skips the signer checks).
- **trusted**: the TEST ONLY root `testdata/pki/trust-anchors.pem` as signing and time-stamping anchor.

All signing uses the TEST ONLY PKI in `testdata/pki`, whose private keys are public.

## Setup

The external validators are separate programs, run as CLIs; nothing is added to `go.mod`. A tool that is not on PATH shows `-` and is never replaced by something else.

1. **Verify c2pa-mcp installs first.** Install it and check that it runs before generating the report:

   ```
   go install github.com/richardwooding/c2pa-mcp@v0.7.1
   c2pa-mcp verify --help
   ```

   If the install or the check fails, stop and fix it; do not substitute the `github.com/richardwooding/c2pa` library.
2. **Add c2patool as the second validator.** It is the Content Authenticity Initiative's official command-line tool (built on c2pa-rs), effectively the reference validator, and this project's oracle. Install a release from <https://github.com/contentauth/c2pa-rs/releases> (or `cargo install c2patool`), then check `c2patool --version`.
3. **Add Credentio as the third validator.** It is Google's C++ C2PA library. It has no releases, so build its `c2pa_validate` tool from the commit pinned in `.github/workflows/interop.yml`, with Clang 19 (libc++) and Bazel as its README describes (`bazel build tools:c2pa_validate`). Put it on PATH as `c2pa_validate` or name it in `CREDENTIO_VALIDATE`, and set `CREDENTIO_COMMIT` to the commit. If it cannot be built, it is shown as not installed, with the reason from `CREDENTIO_BUILD_ERROR`.
4. Run `go run ./interop`.

| c2pa-mcp | v0.7.1 (github.com/richardwooding/c2pa v0.22.1) | run | pure-Go CLI over github.com/richardwooding/c2pa (`verify --json`) |
| Credentio | commit ee4078badeea5739b0f0f246dbaea7803382718b | run | Google's C++ C2PA library (`tools:c2pa_validate`, crJSON output); no releases, built from a pinned commit |
| c2pa-rs library wrapper | - | not run | No wrapper is built; c2patool is the c2pa-rs reference CLI and stands in for it. |

## How results are compared

Tools disagree on their native verdicts by design (c2pa-mcp reports `valid=false` for an untrusted signer, where c2patool reports `Valid`). So each tool's failure codes for the active manifest are reduced to one state with c2pa-rs's rule: any failure other than `signingCredential.untrusted` → **Invalid**; no failures and `signingCredential.trusted` → **Trusted**; otherwise **Valid**; no statuses at all (the tool exited with an error) → **error**. Two tools agree on a file when both the derived state and the set of failure codes match. Success and informational codes are listed per file but not compared.

**Summary:** 104 files × 2 modes = 208 checks; tools agree on 0, disagree on 208 (208 explained below, 0 unexplained).

## Disagreements

### `unsigned/alpha.webp` (default mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Invalid | Invalid | `claim.missing` | - |
| c2patool | error | error: Error: No claim found | - | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Invalid | valid=false | `claim.missing` | - |
| Credentio | error | error: Validation failed: NOT_FOUND: No manifest store found | - | - |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 310 bytes, format webp
- WebP manifest store: webp: no C2PA chunk

**Cause (c2patool: reports an error instead of a status; Credentio: reports an error instead of a status).** The file has no APP11 C2PA segment / no `caBX` chunk (see container facts). c2patool exits with `Error: No claim found` and prints no validation statuses; C2paSeal and c2pa-mcp both report the C2PA failure code `claim.missing`. Not an interoperability problem: all three reject the file. C2paSeal's mapping is required by CLAUDE.md rule 3 (bad input becomes a status code, not an error).

**And (Credentio: reports an error instead of a status).** The file has no APP11 C2PA segment / no `caBX` chunk. Credentio exits with `Validation failed: NOT_FOUND: No manifest store found` and prints no statuses; C2paSeal reports `claim.missing`. Both reject the file.

### `signed/alpha.webp` (default mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | - |
| c2patool | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Valid | valid=false | `signingCredential.untrusted` | `timeStamp.missing`, `signingCredential.revocation.unknown` |
| Credentio | Trusted | crJSON | - | `signingCredential.ocsp.skipped`, `ingredient.unknownProvenance` |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 13894 bytes, format webp
- manifest store: 13576 bytes in a C2PA chunk at file bytes [310, 13894)
- 1 manifest(s); active urn:c2pa:800c97cf-e4c7-4eb7-9618-326bb0f957c7 (claim v2)
- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [310, 13894); exactly the store's bytes: true
- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.opened"
- COSE_Sign1: alg es256, 64-byte signature, 0 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2paSeal Test Signer", valid 2026-10-17T06:20:08Z to 2031-10-16T06:20:08Z

**Cause (Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** In default mode no tool is given trust anchors. c2patool and C2paSeal then check the signer certificate anyway and report it as `signingCredential.untrusted` (and `signingCredential.expired` for `broken/expired.jpg`, whose signer expired with no time-stamp). Credentio's `c2pa_validate` prints `WARNING: --claim_signer_trust is not provided. Skipping claim signer trust checks.` and runs none of them, so it reports no signing-credential status; every other failure is the same as C2paSeal's. Its derived state is therefore Trusted for files the others call Valid. In trusted mode the comparison is meaningful.

### `signed/alpha.webp` (trusted mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Trusted | Trusted | - | - |
| c2patool | Trusted | Trusted | - | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Trusted | valid=true | - | `timeStamp.missing`, `signingCredential.revocation.unknown` |
| Credentio | Invalid | crJSON | `signingCredential.invalid` | - |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 13894 bytes, format webp
- manifest store: 13576 bytes in a C2PA chunk at file bytes [310, 13894)
- 1 manifest(s); active urn:c2pa:800c97cf-e4c7-4eb7-9618-326bb0f957c7 (claim v2)
- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [310, 13894); exactly the store's bytes: true
- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.opened"
- COSE_Sign1: alg es256, 64-byte signature, 0 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2paSeal Test Signer", valid 2026-10-17T06:20:08Z to 2031-10-16T06:20:08Z

**Cause (Credentio: trusts the given anchors only for the `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` EKU).** The signer certificate has the EKUs `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` but not `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` (1.3.6.1.4.1.62558.2.1); this is true of the TEST ONLY PKI and of c2pa-rs's test PKI. C2PA 2.4 §14.5.1.2 asks that a claim signer have at least one EKU for which the validator has trust anchors, and §14.4.1 names `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` as EKUs a user may configure. c2patool and C2paSeal accept them with the anchors given (c2pa-rs's default EKU list). Credentio's `--claim_signer_trust` configures anchors for `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` only and has no option for other EKUs, so it reports `signingCredential.invalid` (`Extended Key Usage c2pa-kp-claimSigning not present`) and stops: it reports none of the file's other failures. Not a defect in the file under the spec; adding `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` to the test PKI would let Credentio check the rest (noted as future work).

All tools agree the token is untrusted. c2patool (c2pa-rs) and C2paSeal log `timeStamp.untrusted` as informational, ignore the token's time and check the signer against the current time, so the manifest stays Valid. c2pa-mcp logs the same code with severity `failure`, which makes the manifest invalid under the common state rule. C2paSeal follows c2patool, the project's oracle (CLAUDE.md rule 4).

**And (Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** In default mode no tool is given trust anchors. c2patool and C2paSeal then check the signer certificate anyway and report it as `signingCredential.untrusted` (and `signingCredential.expired` for `broken/expired.jpg`, whose signer expired with no time-stamp). Credentio's `c2pa_validate` prints `WARNING: --claim_signer_trust is not provided. Skipping claim signer trust checks.` and runs none of them, so it reports no signing-credential status; every other failure is the same as C2paSeal's. Its derived state is therefore Trusted for files the others call Valid. In trusted mode the comparison is meaningful.

**Cause (c2pa-mcp: classifies `timeStamp.untrusted` as a failure; Credentio: trusts the given anchors only for the `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` EKU).** The COSE unprotected header carries one `sigTst2` RFC 3161 token whose TSA certificate does not chain to an anchor that tool was given (container facts name the TSA; in default mode the local test TSA is unknown to every tool, and in trusted mode DigiCert's TSA is not in the test anchors). All tools agree the token is untrusted. c2patool (c2pa-rs) and C2paSeal log `timeStamp.untrusted` as informational, ignore the token's time and check the signer against the current time, so the manifest stays Valid. c2pa-mcp logs the same code with severity `failure`, which makes the manifest invalid under the common state rule. C2paSeal follows c2patool, the project's oracle (CLAUDE.md rule 4).

**And (Credentio: trusts the given anchors only for the `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` EKU).** The signer certificate has the EKUs `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` but not `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` (1.3.6.1.4.1.62558.2.1); this is true of the TEST ONLY PKI and of c2pa-rs's test PKI. C2PA 2.4 §14.5.1.2 asks that a claim signer have at least one EKU for which the validator has trust anchors, and §14.4.1 names `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` as EKUs a user may configure. c2patool and C2paSeal accept them with the anchors given (c2pa-rs's default EKU list). Credentio's `--claim_signer_trust` configures anchors for `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` only and has no option for other EKUs, so it reports `signingCredential.invalid` (`Extended Key Usage c2pa-kp-claimSigning not present`) and stops: it reports none of the file's other failures. Not a defect in the file under the spec; adding `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` to the test PKI would let Credentio check the rest (noted as future work).

### `broken/assertion-flip.jpg` (default mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Invalid | Invalid | `signingCredential.untrusted`, `assertion.hashedURI.mismatch`, `assertion.action.malformed` | - |
| c2patool | Invalid | Invalid | `signingCredential.untrusted`, `assertion.hashedURI.mismatch`, `assertion.action.malformed` | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Invalid | valid=false | `signingCredential.untrusted`, `assertion.hashedURI.mismatch` | `timeStamp.missing`, `signingCredential.revocation.unknown` |
| Credentio | Invalid | crJSON | `assertion.hashedURI.mismatch`, `assertion.undeclared` | `signingCredential.ocsp.skipped` |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 43535 bytes, format jpeg
- manifest store: 42493 bytes in 1 APP11 segment(s) at file bytes [2, 42507)
- 1 manifest(s); active urn:c2pa:19e733df-90ff-45d0-89d4-856088c61fe3 (claim v2)
- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [2, 42507); exactly the store's bytes: true
- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- hashed URI self#jumbf=c2pa.assertions/c2pa.actions.v2: the assertion's SHA-256 does not match the hash recorded in the claim
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.createD"
- COSE_Sign1: alg es256, 64-byte signature, 0 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2paSeal Test Signer", valid 2026-10-17T06:20:08Z to 2031-10-16T06:20:08Z

**Cause (c2pa-mcp: does not evaluate the actions rules here; Credentio: treats an assertion whose hash does not match as undeclared).** `scripts/mkbroken` changed one byte inside the `c2pa.actions.v2` CBOR: the action name `c2pa.created` became `c2pa.createD` (container facts: the actions assertion's hash no longer matches its hashed URI in the claim). All three tools report `assertion.hashedURI.mismatch`. c2patool and C2paSeal also decode the assertion and apply the actions rules (the first action must be `c2pa.created` or `c2pa.opened`), giving `assertion.action.malformed`. c2pa-mcp reports no actions-rule status for this file; whether it skips those rules for mismatched assertions or always, this corpus does not show. Every tool finds the file Invalid.

**And (Credentio: treats an assertion whose hash does not match as undeclared).** `scripts/mkbroken` changed one byte inside an assertion (container facts: its hash no longer matches its hashed URI in the claim): the action name in `c2pa.actions.v2` (`assertion-flip.jpg`), or the recorded hash in `c2pa.hash.data` (`wrong-hash.*`). All tools report `assertion.hashedURI.mismatch`. c2patool and C2paSeal still read the assertion and report what is wrong with it (`assertion.action.malformed`, or `assertion.dataHash.mismatch` from comparing the recorded hash with the image). Credentio drops the assertion as `assertion.undeclared`; without `c2pa.hash.data` the claim has no hard binding, so it reports `claim.hardBindings.missing`. Different codes, same verdict: every tool finds the file Invalid. (In default mode Credentio also skips the signer checks, see above; in trusted mode it stops at the EKU check.)

### `broken/bad-jumbf-length.jpg` (default mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Invalid | Invalid | `claim.malformed` | - |
| c2patool | error | error: Error: claim superbox not found | - | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Invalid | valid=false | `claim.missing` | - |
| Credentio | error | error: Validation failed: NOT_FOUND: could not parse content box for index 0: cannot unwrap content box for index 0: not enough input bytes for declared box size; have 42376 input bytes, box declares its size as 2147483632 bytes | - | - |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 43535 bytes, format jpeg
- 6 JPEG segments; manifest store: jpeg: manifest store: jumbf: parse box at offset 117: truncated box: length 2147483632, 42376 bytes available

**Cause (no oracle: three different answers; no oracle: Credentio reports an error instead of a status).** `scripts/mkbroken` set the 4-byte LBox of the `c2pa.assertions` superbox to `0x7FFFFFF0`, far past the end of the store (container facts give the offset and the claimed versus available length). c2patool cannot find the claim superbox and exits with an error; c2pa-mcp finds `no parseable manifest in store` and reports `claim.missing`; C2paSeal's JUMBF parser rejects the box length and reports `claim.malformed`, because a store is present but its structure is broken. With no status from the oracle this is a judgement call, recorded in PROGRESS.md (Phase 9). All three reject the file.

**And (no oracle: Credentio reports an error instead of a status).** The container or the JUMBF structure is broken by `scripts/mkbroken` (a segment or chunk cut off by the end of the file, or a box length past the end of the store; container facts give the numbers). c2patool also exits with an error here, so there is no oracle status. Credentio exits with a parse error (`truncated JPEG box`, `PNG chunk extends beyond the end of the file`, `RIFF chunk extends beyond the end of the file`, `could not parse content box`); C2paSeal reports `general.error` or `claim.malformed`. Every tool rejects the file.

| c2patool | error | error: Error: claim superbox not found | - | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Invalid | valid=false | `claim.missing` | - |
| Credentio | error | error: Validation failed: NOT_FOUND: could not parse content box for index 0: cannot unwrap content box for index 0: not enough input bytes for declared box size; have 42376 input bytes, box declares its size as 2147483632 bytes | - | - |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 43535 bytes, format jpeg
- 6 JPEG segments; manifest store: jpeg: manifest store: jumbf: parse box at offset 117: truncated box: length 2147483632, 42376 bytes available

**Cause (no oracle: three different answers; no oracle: Credentio reports an error instead of a status).** `scripts/mkbroken` set the 4-byte LBox of the `c2pa.assertions` superbox to `0x7FFFFFF0`, far past the end of the store (container facts give the offset and the claimed versus available length). c2patool cannot find the claim superbox and exits with an error; c2pa-mcp finds `no parseable manifest in store` and reports `claim.missing`; C2paSeal's JUMBF parser rejects the box length and reports `claim.malformed`, because a store is present but its structure is broken. With no status from the oracle this is a judgement call, recorded in PROGRESS.md (Phase 9). All three reject the file.

**And (no oracle: Credentio reports an error instead of a status).** The container or the JUMBF structure is broken by `scripts/mkbroken` (a segment or chunk cut off by the end of the file, or a box length past the end of the store; container facts give the numbers). c2patool also exits with an error here, so there is no oracle status. Credentio exits with a parse error (`truncated JPEG box`, `PNG chunk extends beyond the end of the file`, `RIFF chunk extends beyond the end of the file`, `could not parse content box`); C2paSeal reports `general.error` or `claim.malformed`. Every tool rejects the file.

Credentio's `--claim_signer_trust` configures anchors for `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` only and has no option for other EKUs, so it reports `signingCredential.invalid` (`Extended Key Usage c2pa-kp-claimSigning not present`) and stops: it reports none of the file's other failures. **Cause (Credentio: trusts the given anchors only for the `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` EKU).** The signer certificate has the EKUs `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` but not `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` (1.3.6.1.4.1.62558.2.1); this is true of the TEST ONLY PKI and of c2pa-rs's test PKI. ### `broken/truncated-app11.jpg` (default mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Invalid | Invalid | `general.error` | - |
| c2patool | error | error: Error: asset could not be parsed: Could not parse input JPEG | - | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Invalid | valid=false | `claim.missing` | - |
| Credentio | error | error: Validation failed: INVALID_ARGUMENT: truncated JPEG box | - | - |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 21253 bytes, format jpeg
- JPEG segment walk fails: jpeg: truncated: marker 0xEB at offset 2 needs 42505 bytes, 21251 left

**Cause (no oracle: three different answers; no oracle: Credentio reports an error instead of a status).** `scripts/mkbroken` cut the file in the middle of its manifest store (the first APP11 segment of a JPEG, the `caBX` chunk of a PNG, the `C2PA` chunk of a WebP): its length field declares more bytes than remain (container facts give both numbers), so the file is no longer well formed. c2patool exits with `asset could not be parsed`; c2pa-mcp reports `claim.missing` (`no C2PA manifest found`); C2paSeal's segment or chunk walker fails and `Verify` maps that to `general.error`. All three reject the file; the status code differs because there is no oracle status to follow.

**And (no oracle: Credentio reports an error instead of a status).** The container or the JUMBF structure is broken by `scripts/mkbroken` (a segment or chunk cut off by the end of the file, or a box length past the end of the store; container facts give the numbers). c2patool also exits with an error here, so there is no oracle status. Credentio exits with a parse error (`truncated JPEG box`, `PNG chunk extends beyond the end of the file`, `RIFF chunk extends beyond the end of the file`, `could not parse content box`); C2paSeal reports `general.error` or `claim.malformed`. Every tool rejects the file.

- manifest store: 42493 bytes in 1 APP11 segment(s) at file bytes [2, 42507)

**Cause (Credentio: treats an assertion whose hash does not match as undeclared).** `scripts/mkbroken` changed one byte inside an assertion (container facts: its hash no longer matches its hashed URI in the claim): the action name in `c2pa.actions.v2` (`assertion-flip.jpg`), or the recorded hash in `c2pa.hash.data` (`wrong-hash.*`). All tools report `assertion.hashedURI.mismatch`. c2patool and C2paSeal still read the assertion and report what is wrong with it (`assertion.action.malformed`, or `assertion.dataHash.mismatch` from comparing the recorded hash with the image). Credentio drops the assertion as `assertion.undeclared`; without `c2pa.hash.data` the claim has no hard binding, so it reports `claim.hardBindings.missing`. Different codes, same verdict: every tool finds the file Invalid. (In default mode Credentio also skips the signer checks, see above; in trusted mode it stops at the EKU check.)

| Credentio | Invalid | crJSON | `assertion.hashedURI.mismatch`, `claim.hardBindings.missing`, `assertion.undeclared` | `signingCredential.ocsp.skipped`, `ingredient.unknownProvenance` |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- manifest store: 17867 bytes in a C2PA chunk at file bytes [86, 17962)

All tools report `assertion.hashedURI.mismatch`. c2patool and C2paSeal still read the assertion and report what is wrong with it (`assertion.action.malformed`, or `assertion.dataHash.mismatch` from comparing the recorded hash with the image). Credentio drops the assertion as `assertion.undeclared`; without `c2pa.hash.data` the claim has no hard binding, so it reports `claim.hardBindings.missing`. Different codes, same verdict: every tool finds the file Invalid. (In default mode Credentio also skips the signer checks, see above; in trusted mode it stops at the EKU check.)

**And (Credentio: does not look for manifest stores outside the file).** The file's store is in a `.c2pa` sidecar next to it, or only at the URL in its XMP `dcterms:provenance` (container facts say which). C2PA 2.4 §15.5.3 tells validators to look for both; c2patool and C2paSeal do and agree. Credentio's `c2pa_validate` reads only the asset and exits with `No manifest store found`.

**And (Credentio: does not validate C2PA 1.x manifests).** The active manifest has a C2PA 1.x claim (`c2pa.claim`, label `urn:uuid:…`) or is a pre-1.0 store (container facts give the claim version). c2patool and C2paSeal validate 1.x claims with c2pa-rs's rules (Phase 19). **Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 279782 bytes, format jpeg
- manifest store: 231893 bytes in 4 APP11 segment(s) at file bytes [20, 231985)
- 2 manifest(s); active urn:c2pa:fff4c43d-ffb0-4f23-bd0b-f47f1cf82057:contentauth (claim v2)
- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [20, 231985); exactly the store's bytes: true
- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.opened"
- COSE_Sign1: alg ps256, 512-byte signature, 1 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2PA Signer", valid 2022-06-10T18:46:28Z to 2030-08-26T18:46:28Z
- time-stamp (sigTst2): genTime 2025-10-16T17:31:55Z, TSA "DigiCert SHA256 RSA4096 Timestamp Responder 2025 1" issued by "DigiCert Trusted G4 TimeStamping RSA4096 SHA256 2025 CA1"

**Cause (c2pa-mcp: classifies `timeStamp.untrusted` as a failure; Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** The COSE unprotected header carries one `sigTst2` RFC 3161 token whose TSA certificate does not chain to an anchor that tool was given (container facts name the TSA; in default mode the local test TSA is unknown to every tool, and in trusted mode DigiCert's TSA is not in the test anchors). All tools agree the token is untrusted. c2patool (c2pa-rs) and C2paSeal log `timeStamp.untrusted` as informational, ignore the token's time and check the signer against the current time, so the manifest stays Valid. c2pa-mcp logs the same code with severity `failure`, which makes the manifest invalid under the common state rule. C2paSeal follows c2patool, the project's oracle (CLAUDE.md rule 4).

**And (Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** In default mode no tool is given trust anchors. c2patool and C2paSeal then check the signer certificate anyway and report it as `signingCredential.untrusted` (and `signingCredential.expired` for `broken/expired.jpg`, whose signer expired with no time-stamp). Credentio's `c2pa_validate` prints `WARNING: --claim_signer_trust is not provided. Skipping claim signer trust checks.` and runs none of them, so it reports no signing-credential status; every other failure is the same as C2paSeal's. Its derived state is therefore Trusted for files the others call Valid. In trusted mode the comparison is meaningful.

- size 279782 bytes, format jpeg
- manifest store: 231893 bytes in 4 APP11 segment(s) at file bytes [20, 231985)
- 2 manifest(s); active urn:c2pa:fff4c43d-ffb0-4f23-bd0b-f47f1cf82057:contentauth (claim v2)
- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [20, 231985); exactly the store's bytes: true
- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.opened"
- COSE_Sign1: alg ps256, 512-byte signature, 1 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2PA Signer", valid 2022-06-10T18:46:28Z to 2030-08-26T18:46:28Z
- time-stamp (sigTst2): genTime 2025-10-16T17:31:55Z, TSA "DigiCert SHA256 RSA4096 Timestamp Responder 2025 1" issued by "DigiCert Trusted G4 TimeStamping RSA4096 SHA256 2025 CA1"

**Cause (c2pa-mcp: classifies `timeStamp.untrusted` as a failure; Credentio: trusts the given anchors only for the `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` EKU).** The COSE unprotected header carries one `sigTst2` RFC 3161 token whose TSA certificate does not chain to an anchor that tool was given (container facts name the TSA; in default mode the local test TSA is unknown to every tool, and in trusted mode DigiCert's TSA is not in the test anchors). All tools agree the token is untrusted. c2patool (c2pa-rs) and C2paSeal log `timeStamp.untrusted` as informational, ignore the token's time and check the signer against the current time, so the manifest stays Valid. c2pa-mcp logs the same code with severity `failure`, which makes the manifest invalid under the common state rule. C2paSeal follows c2patool, the project's oracle (CLAUDE.md rule 4).

**And (Credentio: trusts the given anchors only for the `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` EKU).** The signer certificate has the EKUs `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` but not `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` (1.3.6.1.4.1.62558.2.1); this is true of the TEST ONLY PKI and of c2pa-rs's test PKI. C2PA 2.4 §14.5.1.2 asks that a claim signer have at least one EKU for which the validator has trust anchors, and §14.4.1 names `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` as EKUs a user may configure. c2patool and C2paSeal accept them with the anchors given (c2pa-rs's default EKU list). Credentio's `--claim_signer_trust` configures anchors for `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` only and has no option for other EKUs, so it reports `signingCredential.invalid` (`Extended Key Usage c2pa-kp-claimSigning not present`) and stops: it reports none of the file's other failures. Not a defect in the file under the spec; adding `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` to the test PKI would let Credentio check the rest (noted as future work).

**Cause (Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** In default mode no tool is given trust anchors. c2patool and C2paSeal then check the signer certificate anyway and report it as `signingCredential.untrusted` (and `signingCredential.expired` for `broken/expired.jpg`, whose signer expired with no time-stamp). Credentio's `c2pa_validate` prints `WARNING: --claim_signer_trust is not provided. Skipping claim signer trust checks.` and runs none of them, so it reports no signing-credential status; every other failure is the same as C2paSeal's. Its derived state is therefore Trusted for files the others call Valid. In trusted mode the comparison is meaningful.

### `c2paseal/exif.jpg` (default mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | - |
| c2patool | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Valid | valid=false | `signingCredential.untrusted` | `timeStamp.missing`, `signingCredential.revocation.unknown` |
| Credentio | Trusted | crJSON | - | `signingCredential.ocsp.skipped` |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 3317 bytes, format jpeg
- manifest store: 2251 bytes in 1 APP11 segment(s) at file bytes [2, 2265)
- 1 manifest(s); active urn:c2pa:7fbebf52-81df-48b3-99d8-924732fc6d01 (claim v2)
- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [2, 2265); exactly the store's bytes: true
- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.created"
- COSE_Sign1: alg es256, 64-byte signature, 0 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2paSeal Test Signer", valid 2026-10-17T06:20:08Z to 2031-10-16T06:20:08Z

**Cause (Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** In default mode no tool is given trust anchors. c2patool and C2paSeal then check the signer certificate anyway and report it as `signingCredential.untrusted` (and `signingCredential.expired` for `broken/expired.jpg`, whose signer expired with no time-stamp). Credentio's `c2pa_validate` prints `WARNING: --claim_signer_trust is not provided. Skipping claim signer trust checks.` and runs none of them, so it reports no signing-credential status; every other failure is the same as C2paSeal's. Its derived state is therefore Trusted for files the others call Valid. In trusted mode the comparison is meaningful.

### `c2paseal/exif.jpg` (trusted mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Trusted | Trusted | - | - |
| c2patool | Trusted | Trusted | - | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Trusted | valid=true | - | `timeStamp.missing`, `signingCredential.revocation.unknown` |
| Credentio | Invalid | crJSON | `signingCredential.invalid` | - |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 3317 bytes, format jpeg
- manifest store: 2251 bytes in 1 APP11 segment(s) at file bytes [2, 2265)
- 1 manifest(s); active urn:c2pa:7fbebf52-81df-48b3-99d8-924732fc6d01 (claim v2)
- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [2, 2265); exactly the store's bytes: true
- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.created"
- COSE_Sign1: alg es256, 64-byte signature, 0 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2paSeal Test Signer", valid 2026-10-17T06:20:08Z to 2031-10-16T06:20:08Z

**Cause (Credentio: trusts the given anchors only for the `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` EKU).** The signer certificate has the EKUs `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` but not `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` (1.3.6.1.4.1.62558.2.1); this is true of the TEST ONLY PKI and of c2pa-rs's test PKI. C2PA 2.4 §14.5.1.2 asks that a claim signer have at least one EKU for which the validator has trust anchors, and §14.4.1 names `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` as EKUs a user may configure. c2patool and C2paSeal accept them with the anchors given (c2pa-rs's default EKU list). Credentio's `--claim_signer_trust` configures anchors for `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` only and has no option for other EKUs, so it reports `signingCredential.invalid` (`Extended Key Usage c2pa-kp-claimSigning not present`) and stops: it reports none of the file's other failures. Not a defect in the file under the spec; adding `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` to the test PKI would let Credentio check the rest (noted as future work).

- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [2, 12796); exactly the store's bytes: true

**Cause (c2pa-mcp: classifies `timeStamp.untrusted` as a failure; Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** The COSE unprotected header carries one `sigTst2` RFC 3161 token whose TSA certificate does not chain to an anchor that tool was given (container facts name the TSA; in default mode the local test TSA is unknown to every tool, and in trusted mode DigiCert's TSA is not in the test anchors). All tools agree the token is untrusted. c2patool (c2pa-rs) and C2paSeal log `timeStamp.untrusted` as informational, ignore the token's time and check the signer against the current time, so the manifest stays Valid. c2pa-mcp logs the same code with severity `failure`, which makes the manifest invalid under the common state rule. C2paSeal follows c2patool, the project's oracle (CLAUDE.md rule 4).

**And (Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** In default mode no tool is given trust anchors. c2patool and C2paSeal then check the signer certificate anyway and report it as `signingCredential.untrusted` (and `signingCredential.expired` for `broken/expired.jpg`, whose signer expired with no time-stamp). Credentio's `c2pa_validate` prints `WARNING: --claim_signer_trust is not provided. Skipping claim signer trust checks.` and runs none of them, so it reports no signing-credential status; every other failure is the same as C2paSeal's. Its derived state is therefore Trusted for files the others call Valid. In trusted mode the comparison is meaningful.

C2PA 2.4 §15.5.3 tells validators to look for both. c2patool reads the sidecar, or fetches the URL (served by the harness on 127.0.0.1:8765), and C2paSeal does the same (`VerifyFile` finds the sidecar; the harness turns on `FetchRemote`); the two agree. c2pa-mcp `verify` has no sidecar or remote option and reports `claim.missing` (`no C2PA manifest found`).

C2PA 2.4 §15.5.3 tells validators to look for both; c2patool and C2paSeal do and agree. Credentio's `c2pa_validate` reads only the asset and exits with `No manifest store found`.

### `c2paseal/large.jpg` (default mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | - |
| c2patool | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Valid | valid=false | `signingCredential.untrusted` | `timeStamp.missing`, `signingCredential.revocation.unknown` |
| Credentio | Trusted | crJSON | - | `signingCredential.ocsp.skipped` |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 159399 bytes, format jpeg
- manifest store: 2251 bytes in 1 APP11 segment(s) at file bytes [2, 2265)
- 1 manifest(s); active urn:c2pa:f536187d-df8f-42ee-96a2-28bc6508c10f (claim v2)
- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [2, 2265); exactly the store's bytes: true
- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.created"
- COSE_Sign1: alg es256, 64-byte signature, 0 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2paSeal Test Signer", valid 2026-10-17T06:20:08Z to 2031-10-16T06:20:08Z

**Cause (Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** In default mode no tool is given trust anchors. c2patool and C2paSeal then check the signer certificate anyway and report it as `signingCredential.untrusted` (and `signingCredential.expired` for `broken/expired.jpg`, whose signer expired with no time-stamp). Credentio's `c2pa_validate` prints `WARNING: --claim_signer_trust is not provided. Skipping claim signer trust checks.` and runs none of them, so it reports no signing-credential status; every other failure is the same as C2paSeal's. Its derived state is therefore Trusted for files the others call Valid. In trusted mode the comparison is meaningful.

### `c2paseal/large.jpg` (trusted mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Trusted | Trusted | - | - |
| c2patool | Trusted | Trusted | - | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Trusted | valid=true | - | `timeStamp.missing`, `signingCredential.revocation.unknown` |
| Credentio | Invalid | crJSON | `signingCredential.invalid` | - |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 159399 bytes, format jpeg
- manifest store: 2251 bytes in 1 APP11 segment(s) at file bytes [2, 2265)
- 1 manifest(s); active urn:c2pa:f536187d-df8f-42ee-96a2-28bc6508c10f (claim v2)
- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [2, 2265); exactly the store's bytes: true
- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.created"
- COSE_Sign1: alg es256, 64-byte signature, 0 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2paSeal Test Signer", valid 2026-10-17T06:20:08Z to 2031-10-16T06:20:08Z

**Cause (Credentio: trusts the given anchors only for the `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` EKU).** The signer certificate has the EKUs `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` but not `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` (1.3.6.1.4.1.62558.2.1); this is true of the TEST ONLY PKI and of c2pa-rs's test PKI. C2PA 2.4 §14.5.1.2 asks that a claim signer have at least one EKU for which the validator has trust anchors, and §14.4.1 names `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` as EKUs a user may configure. c2patool and C2paSeal accept them with the anchors given (c2pa-rs's default EKU list). Credentio's `--claim_signer_trust` configures anchors for `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` only and has no option for other EKUs, so it reports `signingCredential.invalid` (`Extended Key Usage c2pa-kp-claimSigning not present`) and stops: it reports none of the file's other failures. Not a defect in the file under the spec; adding `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` to the test PKI would let Credentio check the rest (noted as future work).

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- manifest store: 12782 bytes in 1 APP11 segment(s) at file bytes [2, 12796)
- 1 manifest(s); active urn:c2pa:bb4a7303-e6b4-4cf5-879e-61087ec85fe3 (claim v2)
- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [2, 12796); exactly the store's bytes: true
- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.created"
- COSE_Sign1: alg es256, 64-byte signature, 1 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2paSeal Test Signer", valid 2026-10-17T06:20:08Z to 2031-10-16T06:20:08Z

**Cause (c2pa-mcp: classifies `timeStamp.untrusted` as a failure; Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** The COSE unprotected header carries one `sigTst2` RFC 3161 token whose TSA certificate does not chain to an anchor that tool was given (container facts name the TSA; in default mode the local test TSA is unknown to every tool, and in trusted mode DigiCert's TSA is not in the test anchors). All tools agree the token is untrusted. c2patool (c2pa-rs) and C2paSeal log `timeStamp.untrusted` as informational, ignore the token's time and check the signer against the current time, so the manifest stays Valid. c2pa-mcp logs the same code with severity `failure`, which makes the manifest invalid under the common state rule. C2paSeal follows c2patool, the project's oracle (CLAUDE.md rule 4).

**And (Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** In default mode no tool is given trust anchors. c2patool and C2paSeal then check the signer certificate anyway and report it as `signingCredential.untrusted` (and `signingCredential.expired` for `broken/expired.jpg`, whose signer expired with no time-stamp). Credentio's `c2pa_validate` prints `WARNING: --claim_signer_trust is not provided. Skipping claim signer trust checks.` and runs none of them, so it reports no signing-credential status; every other failure is the same as C2paSeal's. Its derived state is therefore Trusted for files the others call Valid. In trusted mode the comparison is meaningful.

- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.created"
- COSE_Sign1: alg es256, 64-byte signature, 0 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2paSeal Test Signer", valid 2026-10-17T06:20:08Z to 2031-10-16T06:20:08Z

**Cause (Credentio: trusts the given anchors only for the `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` EKU).** The signer certificate has the EKUs `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` but not `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` (1.3.6.1.4.1.62558.2.1); this is true of the TEST ONLY PKI and of c2pa-rs's test PKI. C2PA 2.4 §14.5.1.2 asks that a claim signer have at least one EKU for which the validator has trust anchors, and §14.4.1 names `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` as EKUs a user may configure. c2patool and C2paSeal accept them with the anchors given (c2pa-rs's default EKU list). Credentio's `--claim_signer_trust` configures anchors for `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` only and has no option for other EKUs, so it reports `signingCredential.invalid` (`Extended Key Usage c2pa-kp-claimSigning not present`) and stops: it reports none of the file's other failures. Not a defect in the file under the spec; adding `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` to the test PKI would let Credentio check the rest (noted as future work).

### `c2paseal+tsa/large.png` (default mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | `timeStamp.untrusted` |
| c2patool | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | `timeStamp.untrusted` |
| c2pa-mcp | Invalid | valid=false | `timeStamp.untrusted`, `signingCredential.untrusted` | `signingCredential.revocation.unknown` |
| Credentio | Trusted | crJSON | - | `signingCredential.ocsp.skipped` |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 160570 bytes, format png
- manifest store: 12782 bytes in a caBX chunk at file bytes [33, 12827), CRC valid: true
- 1 manifest(s); active urn:c2pa:98853aee-da49-48c3-922a-f42003df38a7 (claim v2)
- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [33, 12827); exactly the store's bytes: true
- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.created"
- COSE_Sign1: alg es256, 64-byte signature, 1 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2paSeal Test Signer", valid 2026-10-17T06:20:08Z to 2031-10-16T06:20:08Z
- time-stamp (sigTst2): genTime 2026-11-14T14:22:11Z, TSA "C2paSeal Test TSA" issued by "C2paSeal Test Root CA"

**Cause (c2pa-mcp: classifies `timeStamp.untrusted` as a failure; Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** The COSE unprotected header carries one `sigTst2` RFC 3161 token whose TSA certificate does not chain to an anchor that tool was given (container facts name the TSA; in default mode the local test TSA is unknown to every tool, and in trusted mode DigiCert's TSA is not in the test anchors). All tools agree the token is untrusted. c2patool (c2pa-rs) and C2paSeal log `timeStamp.untrusted` as informational, ignore the token's time and check the signer against the current time, so the manifest stays Valid. c2pa-mcp logs the same code with severity `failure`, which makes the manifest invalid under the common state rule. C2paSeal follows c2patool, the project's oracle (CLAUDE.md rule 4).

**And (Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** In default mode no tool is given trust anchors. c2patool and C2paSeal then check the signer certificate anyway and report it as `signingCredential.untrusted` (and `signingCredential.expired` for `broken/expired.jpg`, whose signer expired with no time-stamp). Credentio's `c2pa_validate` prints `WARNING: --claim_signer_trust is not provided. Skipping claim signer trust checks.` and runs none of them, so it reports no signing-credential status; every other failure is the same as C2paSeal's. Its derived state is therefore Trusted for files the others call Valid. In trusted mode the comparison is meaningful.

### `c2paseal+sidecar/large.png` (default mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | - |
| c2patool | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Invalid | valid=false | `claim.missing` | - |
| Credentio | error | error: Validation failed: NOT_FOUND: No manifest store found | - | - |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 147776 bytes, format png
- PNG manifest store: png: no C2PA manifest store
- no embedded store; manifest store in sidecar large-png.c2paseal-sidecar.c2pa

**Cause (c2pa-mcp: does not look for manifest stores outside the file; Credentio: does not look for manifest stores outside the file).** The file has no embedded store (no APP11 C2PA segment / no `caBX` chunk); its store is in a `.c2pa` sidecar next to it, or only at the URL in its XMP `dcterms:provenance` (container facts say which). C2PA 2.4 §15.5.3 tells validators to look for both. c2patool reads the sidecar, or fetches the URL (served by the harness on 127.0.0.1:8765), and C2paSeal does the same (`VerifyFile` finds the sidecar; the harness turns on `FetchRemote`); the two agree. c2pa-mcp `verify` has no sidecar or remote option and reports `claim.missing` (`no C2PA manifest found`).

**And (Credentio: does not look for manifest stores outside the file).** The file's store is in a `.c2pa` sidecar next to it, or only at the URL in its XMP `dcterms:provenance` (container facts say which). C2PA 2.4 §15.5.3 tells validators to look for both; c2patool and C2paSeal do and agree. Credentio's `c2pa_validate` reads only the asset and exits with `No manifest store found`.

### `c2paseal+sidecar/large.webp` (default mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | - |
| c2patool | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Invalid | valid=false | `claim.missing` | - |
| Credentio | error | error: Validation failed: NOT_FOUND: No manifest store found | - | - |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 41604 bytes, format webp
- WebP manifest store: webp: no C2PA chunk
- no embedded store; manifest store in sidecar large-webp.c2paseal-sidecar.c2pa

**Cause (c2pa-mcp: does not look for manifest stores outside the file; Credentio: does not look for manifest stores outside the file).** The file has no embedded store (no APP11 C2PA segment / no `caBX` chunk); its store is in a `.c2pa` sidecar next to it, or only at the URL in its XMP `dcterms:provenance` (container facts say which). C2PA 2.4 §15.5.3 tells validators to look for both. c2patool reads the sidecar, or fetches the URL (served by the harness on 127.0.0.1:8765), and C2paSeal does the same (`VerifyFile` finds the sidecar; the harness turns on `FetchRemote`); the two agree. c2pa-mcp `verify` has no sidecar or remote option and reports `claim.missing` (`no C2PA manifest found`).

**And (Credentio: does not look for manifest stores outside the file).** The file's store is in a `.c2pa` sidecar next to it, or only at the URL in its XMP `dcterms:provenance` (container facts say which). C2PA 2.4 §15.5.3 tells validators to look for both; c2patool and C2paSeal do and agree. Credentio's `c2pa_validate` reads only the asset and exits with `No manifest store found`.

C2paSeal follows c2patool, the project's oracle (CLAUDE.md rule 4).

- signer certificate "C2paSeal Test Signer", valid 2026-10-17T06:20:08Z to 2031-10-16T06:20:08Z

**Cause (Credentio: trusts the given anchors only for the `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` EKU).** The signer certificate has the EKUs `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` but not `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` (1.3.6.1.4.1.62558.2.1); this is true of the TEST ONLY PKI and of c2pa-rs's test PKI. C2PA 2.4 §14.5.1.2 asks that a claim signer have at least one EKU for which the validator has trust anchors, and §14.4.1 names `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` as EKUs a user may configure. c2patool and C2paSeal accept them with the anchors given (c2pa-rs's default EKU list). Credentio's `--claim_signer_trust` configures anchors for `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` only and has no option for other EKUs, so it reports `signingCredential.invalid` (`Extended Key Usage c2pa-kp-claimSigning not present`) and stops: it reports none of the file's other failures. Not a defect in the file under the spec; adding `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` to the test PKI would let Credentio check the rest (noted as future work).

### `c2paseal+sidecar/lossy.webp` (default mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | - |
| c2patool | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Invalid | valid=false | `claim.missing` | - |
| Credentio | error | error: Validation failed: NOT_FOUND: No manifest store found | - | - |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 262 bytes, format webp
- WebP manifest store: webp: no C2PA chunk
- no embedded store; manifest store in sidecar lossy-webp.c2paseal-sidecar.c2pa

**Cause (c2pa-mcp: does not look for manifest stores outside the file; Credentio: does not look for manifest stores outside the file).** The file has no embedded store (no APP11 C2PA segment / no `caBX` chunk); its store is in a `.c2pa` sidecar next to it, or only at the URL in its XMP `dcterms:provenance` (container facts say which). C2PA 2.4 §15.5.3 tells validators to look for both. c2patool reads the sidecar, or fetches the URL (served by the harness on 127.0.0.1:8765), and C2paSeal does the same (`VerifyFile` finds the sidecar; the harness turns on `FetchRemote`); the two agree. c2pa-mcp `verify` has no sidecar or remote option and reports `claim.missing` (`no C2PA manifest found`).

**And (Credentio: does not look for manifest stores outside the file).** The file's store is in a `.c2pa` sidecar next to it, or only at the URL in its XMP `dcterms:provenance` (container facts say which). C2PA 2.4 §15.5.3 tells validators to look for both; c2patool and C2paSeal do and agree. Credentio's `c2pa_validate` reads only the asset and exits with `No manifest store found`.

Its derived state is therefore Trusted for files the others call Valid. In trusted mode the comparison is meaningful.

- manifest store: 12782 bytes in 1 APP11 segment(s) at file bytes [2, 12796)
- 1 manifest(s); active urn:c2pa:fa0de530-c9d4-4ce8-a12e-668922b03241 (claim v2)
- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [2, 12796); exactly the store's bytes: true
- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.created"
- COSE_Sign1: alg es256, 64-byte signature, 1 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2paSeal Test Signer", valid 2026-10-17T06:20:08Z to 2031-10-16T06:20:08Z

**Cause (c2pa-mcp: classifies `timeStamp.untrusted` as a failure; Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** The COSE unprotected header carries one `sigTst2` RFC 3161 token whose TSA certificate does not chain to an anchor that tool was given (container facts name the TSA; in default mode the local test TSA is unknown to every tool, and in trusted mode DigiCert's TSA is not in the test anchors). All tools agree the token is untrusted. c2patool (c2pa-rs) and C2paSeal log `timeStamp.untrusted` as informational, ignore the token's time and check the signer against the current time, so the manifest stays Valid. c2pa-mcp logs the same code with severity `failure`, which makes the manifest invalid under the common state rule. C2paSeal follows c2patool, the project's oracle (CLAUDE.md rule 4).

**And (Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** In default mode no tool is given trust anchors. c2patool and C2paSeal then check the signer certificate anyway and report it as `signingCredential.untrusted` (and `signingCredential.expired` for `broken/expired.jpg`, whose signer expired with no time-stamp). Credentio's `c2pa_validate` prints `WARNING: --claim_signer_trust is not provided. Skipping claim signer trust checks.` and runs none of them, so it reports no signing-credential status; every other failure is the same as C2paSeal's. Its derived state is therefore Trusted for files the others call Valid. In trusted mode the comparison is meaningful.

### `c2paseal/small.png` (default mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | - |
| c2patool | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Valid | valid=false | `signingCredential.untrusted` | `timeStamp.missing`, `signingCredential.revocation.unknown` |
| Credentio | Trusted | crJSON | - | `signingCredential.ocsp.skipped` |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 2417 bytes, format png
- manifest store: 2251 bytes in a caBX chunk at file bytes [33, 2296), CRC valid: true
- 1 manifest(s); active urn:c2pa:6a0c9963-7dc0-458a-9bca-19bf32d42bbd (claim v2)
- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [33, 2296); exactly the store's bytes: true
- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.created"
- COSE_Sign1: alg es256, 64-byte signature, 0 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2paSeal Test Signer", valid 2026-10-17T06:20:08Z to 2031-10-16T06:20:08Z

**Cause (Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** In default mode no tool is given trust anchors. c2patool and C2paSeal then check the signer certificate anyway and report it as `signingCredential.untrusted` (and `signingCredential.expired` for `broken/expired.jpg`, whose signer expired with no time-stamp). Credentio's `c2pa_validate` prints `WARNING: --claim_signer_trust is not provided. Skipping claim signer trust checks.` and runs none of them, so it reports no signing-credential status; every other failure is the same as C2paSeal's. Its derived state is therefore Trusted for files the others call Valid. In trusted mode the comparison is meaningful.

### `c2paseal/small.png` (trusted mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Trusted | Trusted | - | - |
| c2patool | Trusted | Trusted | - | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Trusted | valid=true | - | `timeStamp.missing`, `signingCredential.revocation.unknown` |
| Credentio | Invalid | crJSON | `signingCredential.invalid` | - |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 2417 bytes, format png
- manifest store: 2251 bytes in a caBX chunk at file bytes [33, 2296), CRC valid: true
- 1 manifest(s); active urn:c2pa:6a0c9963-7dc0-458a-9bca-19bf32d42bbd (claim v2)
- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [33, 2296); exactly the store's bytes: true
- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.created"
- COSE_Sign1: alg es256, 64-byte signature, 0 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2paSeal Test Signer", valid 2026-10-17T06:20:08Z to 2031-10-16T06:20:08Z

**Cause (Credentio: trusts the given anchors only for the `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` EKU).** The signer certificate has the EKUs `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` but not `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` (1.3.6.1.4.1.62558.2.1); this is true of the TEST ONLY PKI and of c2pa-rs's test PKI. C2PA 2.4 §14.5.1.2 asks that a claim signer have at least one EKU for which the validator has trust anchors, and §14.4.1 names `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` as EKUs a user may configure. c2patool and C2paSeal accept them with the anchors given (c2pa-rs's default EKU list). Credentio's `--claim_signer_trust` configures anchors for `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` only and has no option for other EKUs, so it reports `signingCredential.invalid` (`Extended Key Usage c2pa-kp-claimSigning not present`) and stops: it reports none of the file's other failures. Not a defect in the file under the spec; adding `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` to the test PKI would let Credentio check the rest (noted as future work).

**Cause (c2pa-mcp: classifies `timeStamp.untrusted` as a failure; Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** The COSE unprotected header carries one `sigTst2` RFC 3161 token whose TSA certificate does not chain to an anchor that tool was given (container facts name the TSA; in default mode the local test TSA is unknown to every tool, and in trusted mode DigiCert's TSA is not in the test anchors). All tools agree the token is untrusted. c2patool (c2pa-rs) and C2paSeal log `timeStamp.untrusted` as informational, ignore the token's time and check the signer against the current time, so the manifest stays Valid. c2pa-mcp logs the same code with severity `failure`, which makes the manifest invalid under the common state rule. C2paSeal follows c2patool, the project's oracle (CLAUDE.md rule 4).

**And (Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** In default mode no tool is given trust anchors. c2patool and C2paSeal then check the signer certificate anyway and report it as `signingCredential.untrusted` (and `signingCredential.expired` for `broken/expired.jpg`, whose signer expired with no time-stamp). Skipping claim signer trust checks.` and runs none of them, so it reports no signing-credential status; every other failure is the same as C2paSeal's. Its derived state is therefore Trusted for files the others call Valid. In trusted mode the comparison is meaningful.

**Cause (Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** In default mode no tool is given trust anchors. c2patool and C2paSeal then check the signer certificate anyway and report it as `signingCredential.untrusted` (and `signingCredential.expired` for `broken/expired.jpg`, whose signer expired with no time-stamp). Credentio's `c2pa_validate` prints `WARNING: --claim_signer_trust is not provided. Skipping claim signer trust checks.` and runs none of them, so it reports no signing-credential status; every other failure is the same as C2paSeal's. Its derived state is therefore Trusted for files the others call Valid. In trusted mode the comparison is meaningful.

### `c2paseal/small.webp` (trusted mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Trusted | Trusted | - | - |
| c2patool | Trusted | Trusted | - | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Trusted | valid=true | - | `timeStamp.missing`, `signingCredential.revocation.unknown` |
| Credentio | Invalid | crJSON | `signingCredential.invalid` | - |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 2346 bytes, format webp
- manifest store: 2251 bytes in a C2PA chunk at file bytes [86, 2346)
- 1 manifest(s); active urn:c2pa:658d636a-566e-4e75-81ff-6b9182d1d717 (claim v2)
- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [86, 2345); exactly the store's bytes: false
- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.created"
- COSE_Sign1: alg es256, 64-byte signature, 0 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2paSeal Test Signer", valid 2026-10-17T06:20:08Z to 2031-10-16T06:20:08Z

**Cause (Credentio: trusts the given anchors only for the `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` EKU).** The signer certificate has the EKUs `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` but not `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` (1.3.6.1.4.1.62558.2.1); this is true of the TEST ONLY PKI and of c2pa-rs's test PKI. C2PA 2.4 §14.5.1.2 asks that a claim signer have at least one EKU for which the validator has trust anchors, and §14.4.1 names `id-kp-emailProtection` and `id-kp-documentSigning` as EKUs a user may configure. c2patool and C2paSeal accept them with the anchors given (c2pa-rs's default EKU list). Credentio's `--claim_signer_trust` configures anchors for `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` only and has no option for other EKUs, so it reports `signingCredential.invalid` (`Extended Key Usage c2pa-kp-claimSigning not present`) and stops: it reports none of the file's other failures. Not a defect in the file under the spec; adding `c2pa-kp-claimSigning` to the test PKI would let Credentio check the rest (noted as future work).

### `c2paseal+tsa/small.webp` (default mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | `timeStamp.untrusted` |
| c2patool | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | `timeStamp.untrusted` |
| c2pa-mcp | Invalid | valid=false | `timeStamp.untrusted`, `signingCredential.untrusted` | `signingCredential.revocation.unknown` |
| Credentio | Trusted | crJSON | - | `signingCredential.ocsp.skipped` |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 12876 bytes, format webp
- manifest store: 12782 bytes in a C2PA chunk at file bytes [86, 12876)
- 1 manifest(s); active urn:c2pa:9d9da133-5683-4029-8aa7-24b03d48f89b (claim v2)
- c2pa.hash.data exclusions [86, 12876); exactly the store's bytes: true
- SHA-256 of the file outside the exclusions matches the recorded hash: true
- c2pa.actions.v2: first action "c2pa.created"
- COSE_Sign1: alg es256, 64-byte signature, 1 time-stamp token(s)
- signer certificate "C2paSeal Test Signer", valid 2026-10-17T06:20:08Z to 2031-10-16T06:20:08Z
- time-stamp (sigTst2): genTime 2026-11-14T14:22:11Z, TSA "C2paSeal Test TSA" issued by "C2paSeal Test Root CA"

**Cause (c2pa-mcp: classifies `timeStamp.untrusted` as a failure; Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** The COSE unprotected header carries one `sigTst2` RFC 3161 token whose TSA certificate does not chain to an anchor that tool was given (container facts name the TSA; in default mode the local test TSA is unknown to every tool, and in trusted mode DigiCert's TSA is not in the test anchors). All tools agree the token is untrusted. c2patool (c2pa-rs) and C2paSeal log `timeStamp.untrusted` as informational, ignore the token's time and check the signer against the current time, so the manifest stays Valid. c2pa-mcp logs the same code with severity `failure`, which makes the manifest invalid under the common state rule. C2paSeal follows c2patool, the project's oracle (CLAUDE.md rule 4).

**And (Credentio: skips the signer certificate checks when given no trust anchors).** In default mode no tool is given trust anchors. c2patool and C2paSeal then check the signer certificate anyway and report it as `signingCredential.untrusted` (and `signingCredential.expired` for `broken/expired.jpg`, whose signer expired with no time-stamp). Credentio's `c2pa_validate` prints `WARNING: --claim_signer_trust is not provided. Skipping claim signer trust checks.` and runs none of them, so it reports no signing-credential status; every other failure is the same as C2paSeal's. Its derived state is therefore Trusted for files the others call Valid. In trusted mode the comparison is meaningful.

### `c2paseal+sidecar/small.webp` (default mode)

| Tool | State | Native verdict | Failure | Informational |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C2paSeal | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | - |
| c2patool | Valid | Valid | `signingCredential.untrusted` | - |
| c2pa-mcp | Invalid | valid=false | `claim.missing` | - |
| Credentio | error | error: Validation failed: NOT_FOUND: No manifest store found | - | - |

**Container facts** (computed from the file's bytes):

- size 86 bytes, format webp
- WebP manifest store: webp: no C2PA chunk
- no embedded store; manifest store in sidecar small-webp.c2paseal-sidecar.c2pa

**Cause (c2pa-mcp: does not look for manifest stores outside the file; Credentio: does not look for manifest stores outside the file).** The file has no embedded store (no APP11 C2PA segment / no `caBX` chunk); its store is in a `.c2pa` sidecar next to it, or only at the URL in its XMP `dcterms:provenance` (container facts say which). C2PA 2.4 §15.5.3 tells validators to look for both. c2patool reads the sidecar, or fetches the URL (served by the harness on 127.0.0.1:8765), and C2paSeal does the same (`VerifyFile` finds the sidecar; the harness turns on `FetchRemote`); the two agree. c2pa-mcp `verify` has no sidecar or remote option and reports `claim.missing` (`no C2PA manifest found`).

**And (Credentio: does not look for manifest stores outside the file).** The file's store is in a `.c2pa` sidecar next to it, or only at the URL in its XMP `dcterms:provenance` (container facts say which). C2PA 2.4 §15.5.3 tells validators to look for both; c2patool and C2paSeal do and agree. Credentio's `c2pa_validate` reads only the asset and exits with `No manifest store found`.
